The combination of disrupted sleep, constant worry, and extra shifts to send money home slowly eroded the careful routines he relied on, and coworkers began to notice uncharacteristic mistakes, missed checklists, and tense interactions with supervisors. Over time, his usual meditation and photography outings gave way to ruminative evenings alone, preoccupied with perceived failures toward his family and growing fear that he was letting everyone down, which spread into his relationships at the community center and mosque. After he confided to a trusted community‑center colleague that he often thought his family and neighbors would be better off without him and had started mentally planning ways to disappear, they urged him to seek professional help and accompanied him to a psychiatric clinic.\n- Chief Complaint: “Lately I keep thinking everyone would be better off without me, and I find myself planning how I might just not be here anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Sometimes I’m sure the guys at work and even my relatives back in India are part of some plan to watch me and judge every move I make, like they’re sending each other messages about me through WhatsApp and even through little things on TV, and no matter what people say I can’t shake the feeling it’s all about me.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m alone, especially at night after talking to my family, I hear this deep male voice, not my own, telling me I’m a failure and that I should just go, even though the room is quiet and I know nobody else is there.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): My thoughts get so jumbled that when my supervisor asks a simple question like, ‘Did you finish the fourth floor?’ I start talking about my mother’s blood tests and then jump to the security cameras watching me, and halfway through I realize I never actually answered him.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest, like a dark cloud sitting on me, and it just feels pointless to drag myself through another shift or another phone call about my mother’s health.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to look forward to weekends, going to the park to take photos or sitting at the mosque with the uncles, but now even when they invite me I just say I’m tired and stay in my room staring at the wall because nothing seems worth the effort anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I hardly touch the food I cook and drop a few kilos without trying, and other times I’m so wired and upset I stand in the kitchen at 2 a.m. shoveling rice and sweets into my mouth until I feel sick.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself that my mother’s illness getting worse is my punishment for leaving India, that every small mistake I make at work or at home is proof I’m a terrible son and a useless man who doesn’t deserve any kindness.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): For a long time now, almost every night when I lie down, I find myself thinking it would be better if I just disappeared—imagining walking into the river or stepping in front of a train so my family wouldn’t have to worry about me anymore and my coworkers wouldn’t have to cover for my mistakes.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It swings on me: some weeks I feel strangely high and touchy at the same time, cracking jokes too loud at work and then snapping in anger if someone criticizes me even a little, and other weeks I just feel flat and low like there’s nothing left inside.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into that ‘up’ phase, I can’t sit still at all—I’ll scrub the same hallway three times, reorganize the supply closet at 3 a.m., then rush home and start cleaning my whole apartment or making lists for my family in India, like I’m powered by some motor I can’t switch off.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are times I’m absolutely convinced I’m the only one who can fix everything—that I can somehow cure my mother from here and also run the whole building better than the managers, like I’ve been chosen for some special mission and everyone else is just too slow to see it.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On those wired nights, I’ll sleep maybe two or three hours, if at all, but I don’t feel tired; instead I feel like sleep is a waste of time and I should be on the phone managing doctors in India or redoing work checklists because my mind won’t let me rest.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My head races so fast that one second I’m thinking about sending money, then suddenly I’m planning a new business, worrying about spies in the building, and remembering a mistake I made in high school, all crashing into each other so I can’t focus on any one thing or calm myself down.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in that speedy mode at work, every little sound or movement—someone dropping a mop, a light flickering, a text buzz on my phone—pulls my attention away so I leave tasks half done and keep bouncing from one thing to another without finishing.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In my high periods I start a hundred ‘important’ projects at once—calling distant relatives in the middle of the night to give them advice, offering to take extra shifts, planning big donations at the mosque I can’t afford, even messaging old girlfriends—until I wear people out and then crash hard afterward.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I get into those wired, high phases where I’m taking extra shifts and barely sleeping, that same deep voice telling me I’m a failure gets louder and the feeling that everyone is sending secret messages about me through their phones and the TV becomes almost constant.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over these last couple of years, most of the time I’ve either been in that sped‑up mode—working extra, sleeping just a few hours, starting big plans—or just coming down from it, with only shorter stretches where I felt more or less in the middle.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch a few months ago when my mood felt more ‘normal,’ not especially high or low, but I was still sure people at work and back home were coordinating against me and I kept hearing that man’s voice at night even though I wasn’t particularly depressed or excited.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, my work has suffered so much that my hours were cut and I’m on a final warning, I’ve mostly stopped going to the mosque and community center, and at home I let bills and basic chores pile up until someone reminds me or I get a notice.\n- duration: Ever since my mother first became seriously ill back home a little over two years ago, these mood swings and strange experiences have been affecting me on and off without really going back to how I was before.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The clear sense that people are watching and judging me and the nighttime voice talking to me have been a regular problem for about a year and a half now.\n- manic_episode_duration: Those wired, restless periods where I sleep very little, take on too much, and feel like I’m on some special mission have been coming and going for roughly the last year and eight months.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavier, low stretches when I feel worthless and think about disappearing have been recurring on and off for about seven months in total.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in and stay in my room staring at the wall because nothing seems worth the effort anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I hardly touch the food I cook and drop a few kilos without trying, and other times I’m so wired and upset I stand in the kitchen at 2 a.m. shoveling rice and sweets into my mouth until I feel sick.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself that my mother’s illness getting worse is my punishment for leaving India, that every small mistake I make at work or at home is proof I’m a terrible son and a useless man who doesn’t deserve any kindness.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): For a long time now, almost every night when I lie down, I find myself thinking it would be better if I just disappeared—imagining walking into the river or stepping in front of a train so my family wouldn’t have to worry about me anymore and my coworkers wouldn’t have to cover for my mistakes.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It swings on me: some weeks I feel strangely high and touchy at the same time, cracking jokes too loud at work and then snapping in anger if someone criticizes me even a little, and other weeks I just feel flat and low like there’s nothing left inside.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into that ‘up’ phase, I can’t sit still at all—I’ll scrub the same hallway three times, reorganize the supply closet at 3 a.m., then rush home and start cleaning my whole apartment or making lists for my family in India, like I’m powered by some motor I can’t switch off.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are times I’m absolutely convinced I’m the only one who can fix everything—that I can somehow cure my mother from here and also run the whole building better than the managers, like I’ve been chosen for some special mission and everyone else is just too slow to see it.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On those wired nights, I’ll sleep maybe two or three hours, if at all, but I don’t feel tired; instead I feel like sleep is a waste of time and I should be on the phone managing doctors in India or redoing work checklists because my mind won’t let me rest.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My head races so fast that one second I’m thinking about sending money, then suddenly I’m planning a new business, worrying about spies in the building, and remembering a mistake I made in high school, all crashing into each other so I can’t focus on any one thing or calm myself down.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in that speedy mode at work, every little sound or movement—someone dropping a mop, a light flickering, a text buzz on my phone—pulls my attention away so I leave tasks half done and keep bouncing from one thing to another without finishing.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In my high periods I start a hundred ‘important’ projects at once—calling distant relatives in the middle of the night to give them advice, offering to take extra shifts, planning big donations at the mosque I can’t afford, even messaging old girlfriends—until I wear people out and then crash hard afterward.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I get into those wired, high phases where I’m taking extra shifts and barely sleeping, that same deep voice telling me I’m a failure gets louder and the feeling that everyone is sending secret messages about me through their phones and the TV becomes almost constant.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over these last couple of years, most of the time I’ve either been in that sped‑up mode—working extra, sleeping just a few hours, starting big plans—or just coming down from it, with only shorter stretches where I felt more or less in the middle.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch a few months ago when my mood felt more ‘normal,’ not especially high or low, but I Before these last seven months, I had never experienced these heavy, hopeless feelings or thought about disappearing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past seven months, have you noticed significant changes in your sleep, appetite, or your ability to concentrate on daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been completely all over the place. Some nights I only sleep two or three hours without feeling tired, and my eating swings from barely touching food to binging on rice and sweets at 2 a.m. Also, my concentration at work is terrible because I get so easily distracted by every little thing and leave tasks half-done.\n\nDoctor: During those periods where you were sleeping very little and feeling highly distracted, did you also feel unusually energetic, talkative, or feel like your thoughts were racing faster than normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those times my head races incredibly fast with thoughts constantly crashing into each other. I feel this restless energy like I am powered by a motor I can't turn off, so I can't sit still and end up cleaning or starting multiple projects while my mind jumps around.\n\nDoctor: During those periods of high energy and racing thoughts, have you ever felt so out of control or disconnected from reality that you required hospitalization or had experiences that others couldn't see or hear?\n\nPatient: I have never been hospitalized, but during those wired phases, I definitely experience things that others don't. The deep male voice telling me I'm a failure gets much louder, and the feeling that people are sending secret messages about me through their phones and the TV becomes almost constant."

I feel this restless energy like I am powered by a motor I can't turn off, so I can't sit still and end up cleaning or starting multiple projects while my mind jumps around.\n\nDoctor: During those periods of high energy and racing thoughts, have you ever felt so out of control or disconnected from reality that you required hospitalization or had experiences that others couldn't see or hear?\n\nPatient: I have never been hospitalized, but during those wired phases, I definitely experience things that others don't. The deep male voice telling me I'm a failure gets much louder, and the feeling that people are sending secret messages about me through their phones and the TV becomes almost constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these voices and feelings of being watched only during the periods when your mood is very high or very low, or do they also happen when your mood is relatively stable?\n\nPatient: Yes, they actually do happen even when my mood is relatively stable. For example, a few months ago when my mood felt more normal and neither high nor low, I was still convinced people were coordinating against me and I kept hearing that deep male voice at night."

But these specific heavy, low feelings and the thoughts of disappearing have really been happening on and off for about seven months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any similar periods of feeling this depressed or hopeless before these last seven months, or is this the first time you have felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is actually the first time in my life I've ever felt this specific kind of deep depression. Before these last seven months, I had never experienced these heavy, hopeless feelings or thought about disappearing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past seven months, have you noticed significant changes in your sleep, appetite, or your ability to concentrate on daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been completely all over the place. Some nights I only sleep two or three hours without feeling tired, and my eating swings from barely touching food to binging on rice and sweets at 2 a.m. Also, my concentration at work is terrible because I get so easily distracted by every little thing and leave tasks half-done.\n\nDoctor: During those periods where you were sleeping very little and feeling highly distracted, did you also feel unusually energetic, talkative, or feel like your thoughts were racing faster than normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those times my head races incredibly fast with thoughts constantly crashing into each other. I feel this restless energy like I am powered by a motor I can't turn off, so I can't sit still and end up cleaning or starting multiple projects while my mind jumps around.\n\nDoctor: During those periods of high energy and racing thoughts, have you ever felt so out of control or disconnected from reality that you required hospitalization or had experiences that others couldn't see or hear?\n\nPatient: I have never been hospitalized, but during those wired phases, I definitely experience things that others don't. The deep male voice telling me I'm a failure gets much louder, and the feeling that people are sending secret messages about me through their phones and the TV becomes almost constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these voices and feelings of being watched only during the periods when your mood is very high or very low, or do they also happen when your mood is relatively stable?\n\nPatient: Yes, they actually do happen even when my mood is relatively stable. For example, a few months ago when my mood felt more normal and neither high nor low, I was still convinced people were coordinating against me and I kept hearing that deep male voice at night.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, do you experience these voices and paranoid thoughts for at least two weeks in the absence of any significant mood symptoms (neither high nor low)?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is exactly what happened. A few months ago, there was a stretch of about two or three weeks where my mood felt completely normal, not high or low at all. But even during those weeks, I was still hearing that deep voice at night and felt sure people were coordinating against me."

Before these last seven months, I had never experienced these heavy, hopeless feelings or thought about disappearing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past seven months, have you noticed significant changes in your sleep, appetite, or your ability to concentrate on daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been completely all over the place. Some nights I only sleep two or three hours without feeling tired, and my eating swings from barely touching food to binging on rice and sweets at 2 a.m. Also, my concentration at work is terrible because I get so easily distracted by every little thing and leave tasks half-done.\n\nDoctor: During those periods where you were sleeping very little and feeling highly distracted, did you also feel unusually energetic, talkative, or feel like your thoughts were racing faster than normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those times my head races incredibly fast with thoughts constantly crashing into each other. I feel this restless energy like I am powered by a motor I can't turn off, so I can't sit still and end up cleaning or starting multiple projects while my mind jumps around.\n\nDoctor: During those periods of high energy and racing thoughts, have you ever felt so out of control or disconnected from reality that you required hospitalization or had experiences that others couldn't see or hear?\n\nPatient: I have never been hospitalized, but during those wired phases, I definitely experience things that others don't. The deep male voice telling me I'm a failure gets much louder, and the feeling that people are sending secret messages about me through their phones and the TV becomes almost constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these voices and feelings of being watched only during the periods when your mood is very high or very low, or do they also happen when your mood is relatively stable?\n\nPatient: Yes, they actually do happen even when my mood is relatively stable. For example, a few months ago when my mood felt more normal and neither high nor low, I was still convinced people were coordinating against me and I kept hearing that deep male voice at night.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, do you experience these voices and paranoid thoughts for at least two weeks in the absence of any significant mood symptoms (neither high nor low)?\n\nPatient: Yes, that is exactly what happened. A few months ago, there was a stretch of about two or three weeks where my mood felt completely normal, not high or low at all. But even during those weeks, I was still hearing that deep voice at night and felt sure people were coordinating against me.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the entire seven-month period, have the symptoms of your mood swings (the high energy or the deep depression) been present for the majority of the total duration of your illness?\n\nPatient: Yes, looking back over the last couple of years, most of my time has been spent in that sped-up, high-energy mode or recovering from it. I have only had much shorter stretches where I felt normal or somewhere in the middle.\n\nDoctor: Over the seven-month period, would you say you have spent more time experiencing the high-energy, 'sped-up' symptoms, or have you spent more time experiencing the deep, hopeless depression?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly, I have spent much more of my time in those sped-up, high-energy periods. While the heavy, depressed feelings have been coming and going for about seven months, those wired phases have actually been going on much longer—for about a year and eight months now. They have made up the vast majority of my time over these last couple of years.

Over time, his usual meditation and photography outings gave way to ruminative evenings alone, preoccupied with perceived failures toward his family and growing fear that he was letting everyone down, which spread into his relationships at the community center and mosque. After he confided to a trusted community‑center colleague that he often thought his family and neighbors would be better off without him and had started mentally planning ways to disappear, they urged him to seek professional help and accompanied him to a psychiatric clinic.\n- Chief Complaint: “Lately I keep thinking everyone would be better off without me, and I find myself planning how I might just not be here anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Sometimes I’m sure the guys at work and even my relatives back in India are part of some plan to watch me and judge every move I make, like they’re sending each other messages about me through WhatsApp and even through little things on TV, and no matter what people say I can’t shake the feeling it’s all about and stay in my room staring at the wall because nothing seems worth the effort anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I hardly touch the food I (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself that my mother’s illness getting worse is my punishment for leaving India, that every small mistake I make at work or at home is proof I’m a terrible son disappeared—imagining walking into the river or stepping in front of a train so my family wouldn’t have to worry about me anymore and my coworkers wouldn’t have to cover for my mistakes.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It swings on me: some weeks I feel strangely high and touchy at the same time, cracking jokes too loud at work and then snapping in anger if someone criticizes me even a little, and other weeks I just feel flat and low like there’s nothing left inside.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into that ‘up’ phase, I can’t sit still at all—I’ll scrub the same hallway Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
